response:
  reply: '# Draft dossier: Harbor Pay acquisition migration


    **Status:** Working draft. It uses the hypothetical assumption that a partner
    bank holds customer funds. It does not change the current transaction plan or
    record a decision.


    ## Executive summary


    The Harbor migration needs legal and compliance review before the planned November
    15 close.


    Mosaic should not:


    - assume that Harbor’s state licenses transfer in the asset purchase;

    - launch in the four Harbor-only states without confirmed authority;

    - rely on Harbor’s prior identity checks until compliance approves that approach;

    - migrate customers linked to unresolved alerts without review; or

    - depend on an untested day-one monitoring switch.


    A partner bank holding funds does not resolve these issues by itself. The key
    question is who provides the service, controls transfers, and owes the customer.


    **Working recommendation:** Separate the acquisition closing from the launch of
    regulated services. Block affected services where authority or compliance readiness
    is not confirmed.


    ## Reported facts


    - Mosaic is buying Harbor’s assets.

    - Closing is targeted for November 15.

    - About 240,000 consumers and 12,000 business accounts will move on day one.

    - Users will receive one account, one balance, and access to Mosaic’s products.

    - Mosaic plans no customer re-verification.

    - Pass/fail identity-check results exist, but some pre-2023 supporting documents
    are missing.

    - Harbor’s transaction-monitoring contract ends at close.

    - About 40 alerts and several suspicious activity reports remain unread.

    - Mosaic plans to use or assume Harbor’s licenses in four additional states.

    - Marketing proposes a $50 first-transfer bonus.


    ## Hypothetical assumption


    A partner bank holds customer funds.


    This does not establish that the bank is the legal service provider. That depends
    on the contracts and actual operations, including:


    - who contracts with users;

    - who accepts transfer instructions;

    - who controls settlement;

    - who records balances;

    - who owes refunds;

    - who handles complaints and losses; and

    - who performs customer checks, monitoring, alert review, and reporting.


    ## Main issues


    ### State licensing


    Harbor’s licenses should not be treated as assets that automatically move to Mosaic.


    The four Harbor-only states present the clearest launch risk. Mosaic should require
    its own authority, an approved transition, or another confirmed legal basis before
    offering regulated services there.


    The state review should cover asset-sale notices, buyer applications, changes
    in business plans, customer-obligation transfers, bonds, interim operations, and
    license surrender.


    **Support limit:** The four states have not been identified. No current state-specific
    rule or regulator instruction has been verified for this transaction.


    ### Partner-bank structure


    Bank custody can affect the analysis, but it is not automatically a licensing
    exemption.


    A genuine bank-led program could produce a different result if the bank is the
    actual provider and Mosaic performs only permitted support services. The contracts,
    customer terms, account control, and operations must all support that structure.


    ### Customer identification records


    Compliance has not confirmed whether Mosaic’s program permits reliance on Harbor’s
    prior checks.


    Before migration, compliance should determine:


    - whether reliance is permitted;

    - which records Mosaic must hold;

    - which customers need new checks;

    - whether missing records can be repaired; and

    - whether affected accounts need temporary restrictions.


    ### Alerts and suspicious activity reports


    The unread alerts and report history are a pre-close diligence priority.


    The review could identify unresolved suspicious activity, reporting gaps, restricted
    customers, or broader weaknesses in Harbor’s compliance program. Findings could
    affect customer migration, closing conditions, deal protections, or price.


    ### Monitoring transition


    Repointing Harbor’s transaction feed on day one does not prove that monitoring
    will work.


    Mosaic should test:


    - data completeness;

    - customer-history transfer;

    - monitoring-rule coverage;

    - alert generation;

    - case routing;

    - sanctions controls;

    - escalation procedures; and

    - the fallback process if the connection fails.


    ### Customer terms and communications


    The unified Mosaic account may change the service provider, contract, product
    set, fees, privacy terms, and complaint contact.


    Legal should review contract assignment, required notices, consent, balance presentation,
    privacy disclosures, and statements about the partner bank.


    ### Welcome bonus


    The $50 campaign needs review when its final terms are available.


    Marketing must define eligibility, qualifying transfers, payment timing, exclusions,
    expiration, and disclosures. The offer should not launch until the advertised
    benefit matches the actual product rules.


    ## Recommended pre-close conditions


    1. Identify the four Harbor-only states.

    2. Complete a state-by-state authority and filing matrix.

    3. Confirm the legal service provider after closing.

    4. Document the full funds and customer-obligation flow.

    5. Review the partner-bank agreements and required consents.

    6. Review the open alerts and suspicious activity report history.

    7. Decide how to handle customers with missing identity records.

    8. Test the monitoring transition and fallback.

    9. confirm customer contract and notice requirements.

    10. Add state and customer restrictions where needed.


    ## Fallback paths


    **State launch hold:** Close the acquisition, but block regulated services where
    Mosaic lacks confirmed authority.


    **Harbor transition:** Keep Harbor as the actual licensed provider temporarily,
    where the contracts, regulators, bank, and operations permit it.


    **Bank-led program:** Make the partner bank the actual provider. This would require
    material changes to contracts, controls, customer disclosures, and operations.


    ## Working conclusion


    The acquisition can potentially close before every customer is migrated. Mosaic
    should treat legal authority and compliance readiness as launch conditions.


    The safest working rule is:


    > **No confirmed authority and no tested compliance coverage means no day-one
    regulated service for the affected customer.**


    The next step is to identify the four Harbor-only states and prepare one combined
    licensing, funds-flow, and compliance-readiness matrix.'

    # original_request_historical_not_current_facts: original_request

    # Original Request


    From: Dana, PM — Growth & Integration

    Subject: Harbor migration — anything here need legal?


    We''re acquiring Harbor Pay. Close is targeted Nov 15 (~10 weeks). Migration plan:


    240k consumer users, 12k business accounts move into our app on day 1 post-close.
    One unified account, one balance, full access to our product set.

    No re-verification — they were already KYC''d by Harbor.

    Harbor holds MTLs in 38 states, we hold 41. Combined that''s 44 — nice win, opens
    four new markets.

    Harbor''s KYC ran through a vendor that shut down in 2024. We have pass/fail results
    for everyone, but not the underlying documents for users onboarded before 2023.

    Harbor''s monitoring is a third-party SaaS whose contract terminates at close.
    Plan is to repoint their transaction feed at our engine on day 1.

    Marketing wants a "Welcome" campaign with a $50 bonus on first transfer, launching
    close+1.

    Diligence flagged that Harbor has ~40 open alerts and "a handful" of SARs from
    the last year. Nobody''s read them yet.


    Does any of this need legal before close? It''s really just a data migration.



    ---

    # company.md

    # Company profile


    ## Company Name


    Mosaic Relay


    ## Website Url


    Not provided.


    ## Summary


    Mosaic Relay is a fictional payment infrastructure company created only for this
    Counsel OS UX experiment. It has no real-company affiliation and must not be confused
    with Stripe, Checkout.com, Marqeta, Manifest Financial, Chime, or any other real
    company. Business model: Mosaic Relay sells API and hosted infrastructure to online
    businesses, marketplaces, and software platforms. It charges platform and usage
    fees for payment acceptance, risk, operations, and payout tools. It is not a bank.
    It does not offer bank accounts, hold deposits, take deposits, or operate as a
    bank. Products and services: APIs and hosted tools for card, ACH, and local payment
    acceptance; payment routing and splits; refunds and disputes; seller, contractor,
    and recipient payouts; merchant onboarding; identity verification; KYB and KYC;
    sanctions screening; fraud monitoring; transaction risk scoring; payout controls;
    reconciliation; and operations dashboards. Customers: online businesses, marketplaces,
    and software platforms, including businesses that onboard sellers, contractors,
    or recipients. Mosaic Relay supports their payment operations but does not take
    custody of customer deposits. Partners and dependencies: acquiring banks, payment
    processors, card networks, identity and fraud vendors, and cloud providers. These
    are third-party relationships, not ownership or affiliation. Footprint and teams:
    fictional US-headquartered company serving customers in North America, the UK,
    EU, and selected other markets through remote and regional teams. Core teams are
    product and engineering, payments operations, risk and trust, compliance and financial
    crime, sales and customer success, security and privacy, finance, and legal. Legal
    topics: BSA/AML and financial-crime controls; money transmission and payments
    regulation; card-network and processor rules; ACH and local-payment rules; consumer
    protection and funds-flow disclosures; privacy and data protection; cybersecurity
    and incident response; sanctions; KYB/KYC and identity; vendor and cloud contracts;
    commercial terms; IP; employment; and marketing compliance. Risk posture: moderate-to-high
    regulatory and operational risk because the platform supports payment acceptance,
    onboarding, risk decisions, and payouts. Mosaic Relay uses risk-based controls,
    clear ownership, documented escalation, auditability, vendor oversight, data minimization,
    and human review for material exceptions. It aims to prevent fraud, sanctions
    breaches, unsafe payouts, and misleading customer claims while keeping the product
    usable. Operating principles: be accurate about what Mosaic Relay does and does
    not do; separate recommendations from recorded decisions; state material assumptions;
    preserve an editable work product; avoid claiming licenses, registrations, approvals,
    or bank status unless confirmed; protect personal data; and favor a useful first
    pass with clear gaps over a blank result. Fictional legal owner: Jordan Lee, fictional
    General Counsel and Head of Regulatory Affairs for Mosaic Relay. Jordan owns legal
    decisions and escalation. The company, people, partners, customers, and footprint
    described here are fictional for testing.


    ## Business Model


    Sells API and hosted infrastructure to online businesses, marketplaces, and software
    platforms. Charges platform and usage fees for payment acceptance, risk, operations,
    and payout tools. Not a bank; does not hold or take deposits.


    ## Products Services


    APIs and hosted tools for card, ACH, and local payment acceptance; payment routing
    and splits; refunds and disputes; seller, contractor, and recipient payouts; merchant
    onboarding; identity verification; KYB and KYC; sanctions screening; fraud monitoring;
    transaction risk scoring; payout controls; reconciliation; and operations dashboards.


    ## Jurisdictions


    US-headquartered; serves customers in North America, the UK, EU, and selected
    other markets through remote and regional teams.


    ## Regulatory Context


    Subject to BSA/AML and financial-crime controls; money transmission and payments
    regulation; card-network and processor rules; ACH and local-payment rules; consumer
    protection and funds-flow disclosures; privacy and data protection; cybersecurity
    and incident response; sanctions; KYB/KYC and identity; vendor and cloud contracts;
    commercial terms; IP; employment; and marketing compliance.


    ## Data Practices


    Processes personal data in connection with identity verification, KYB/KYC, sanctions
    screening, fraud monitoring, and transaction risk scoring. Emphasizes data minimization
    and protection of personal data.


    ## Risk Posture


    Moderate-to-high regulatory and operational risk due to payment acceptance, onboarding,
    risk decisions, and payouts. Uses risk-based controls, clear ownership, documented
    escalation, auditability, vendor oversight, data minimization, and human review
    for material exceptions. Aims to prevent fraud, sanctions breaches, unsafe payouts,
    and misleading customer claims while keeping the product usable.

        inputs:
          business_question: &id001
            question_id: BQ-c9d7c39f6380eb60
            text: Does any part of the Harbor Pay acquisition migration need legal
              review before the Nov 15 close?
            revision: legacy:6abf4a45ae87313af8db04b89d546e0e1a1c6137341b3d3c0d159c66cf64d54c
            dossier_revision: a0c90a373f8cf91351373874a70856bf77a2e51bdd276b26f0870804a57a2351
            origin: legacy_unknown
            source_message_id: null
            source_action_key: null
            updated_at: null
          issue:
            issue_id: ISS-c9d7c39f63-94e42e2b38090045
            title: 'Money transmission licensing: whether Mosaic Relay can operate
              under the combined 44-state footprint and whether Harbor''s MTLs transfer/survive
              the acquisition'
            why_it_matters: ''
            parent_issue_id: null
            fact_ids: []
          facts:
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-182bcc
            text: Mosaic Relay is acquiring Harbor Pay; close targeted Nov 15 (~10
              weeks out).
            source_ids: &id002
            - REQ-20260909-283892
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-551367
            text: Migration moves 240k consumer users and 12k business accounts into
              Mosaic Relay's app on day 1 post-close, with one unified account and
              balance and full access to Mosaic's product set.
            source_ids: &id003
            - REQ-20260909-283892
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-9a0c61
            text: Plan is no re-verification; Harbor users were already KYC'd by Harbor.
            source_ids: &id004
            - REQ-20260909-283892
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-33293c
            text: Harbor holds money transmitter licenses (MTLs) in 38 states; Mosaic
              Relay holds 41; combined footprint is 44 states, opening four new markets.
            source_ids: &id005
            - REQ-20260909-283892
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-aea8ec
            text: Harbor's KYC ran through a vendor that shut down in 2024; Mosaic
              has pass/fail results for everyone but not the underlying documents
              for users onboarded before 2023.
            source_ids: &id006
            - REQ-20260909-283892
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-073838
            text: Harbor's transaction monitoring is a third-party SaaS whose contract
              terminates at close; plan is to repoint Harbor's transaction feed at
              Mosaic's engine on day 1.
            source_ids: &id007
            - REQ-20260909-283892
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-19aebc
            text: Marketing wants a 'Welcome' campaign with a $50 bonus on first transfer,
              launching close+1.
            source_ids: &id008
            - REQ-20260909-283892
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-adf2cb
            text: Diligence flagged ~40 open alerts and 'a handful' of SARs from the
              last year at Harbor; nobody has read them yet.
            source_ids: &id009
            - REQ-20260909-283892
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-4c1fd8
            text: Migration moves 240k consumer users and 12k business accounts into
              Mosaic Relay's app on day 1 post-close, with one unified account and
              balance.
            source_ids: &id010
            - MSG-20260909-9835ba
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-f2c49f
            text: Plan is no re-verification of Harbor's existing KYC; Harbor users
              were already KYC'd.
            source_ids: &id011
            - MSG-20260909-9835ba
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-13d4c1
            text: Harbor's KYC ran through a vendor that shut down in 2024; pass/fail
              results exist for everyone but underlying documents are missing for
              users onboarded before 2023.
            source_ids: &id012
            - MSG-20260909-9835ba
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-78d5cd
            text: Harbor's transaction monitoring is a third-party SaaS whose contract
              terminates at close; plan is to repoint the transaction feed to Mosaic
              Relay's engine on day 1.
            source_ids: &id013
            - MSG-20260909-9835ba
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-9f7bde
            text: Diligence flagged ~40 open alerts and 'a handful' of SARs from the
              last year at Harbor; none have been read yet.
            source_ids: &id014
            - MSG-20260909-9835ba
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-ed9f06
            text: How is the Harbor Pay acquisition structured? — Asset purchase (Mosaic
              buys Harbor's assets)
            source_ids: &id015
            - MSG-20260909-2e549e
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-b5e71e
            text: Does Mosaic Relay's AML/CIP program currently permit relying on
              another institution's prior KYC without re-verification, or is re-verification
              required? — Not sure / need to check with compliance
            source_ids: &id016
            - MSG-20260909-2e549e
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-849b00
            text: What is known about Harbor's ~40 open alerts and the SARs from the
              last year? — Nothing reviewed yet — still in diligence
            source_ids: &id017
            - MSG-20260909-2e549e
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-f0fb01
            text: For the four new markets opened by the combined footprint, does
              Mosaic Relay plan to operate under Harbor's existing licenses or apply
              for its own? — Use/assume Harbor's licenses
            source_ids: &id018
            - MSG-20260909-2e549e
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-620c62
            text: For the pre-2023 Harbor users with no underlying KYC documents,
              what is the plan to satisfy Mosaic Relay's own BSA/AML customer identification
              and recordkeeping obligations? — It is not yet known whether Mosaic
              Relay's AML/CIP program permits reliance on Harbor's prior KYC; compliance
              must confirm.
            source_ids: &id019
            - MSG-20260909-2e549e
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-070aec
            text: Have the ~40 open alerts and the handful of SARs from the last year
              been reviewed, and what did they reveal? — The open alerts and SAR history
              have not been reviewed and remain in diligence.
            source_ids: &id020
            - MSG-20260909-2e549e
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-771ee1
            text: For the four new markets opened by the combined license footprint,
              what is the plan for state money transmission licensing or approval
              before onboarding those customers? — Mosaic Relay currently plans to
              use or assume Harbor's licenses for the four new markets.
            source_ids: &id021
            - MSG-20260909-2e549e
          assumptions:
          - assumption_id: ASM-20260909-29b823
            text: Mosaic Relay's existing money transmission licenses and Harbor's
              MTLs are transferable or can be maintained through the acquisition without
              new state approval
            reason: Needed to continue
          - assumption_id: ASM-20260909-3c5756
            text: The KYC pass/fail results are sufficient to satisfy Mosaic Relay's
              own BSA/AML program obligations without the underlying documents
            reason: Needed to continue
          - assumption_id: ASM-20260909-1a519f
            text: The transaction monitoring repoint can be completed and validated
              before close without a monitoring gap
            reason: Needed to continue
          - assumption_id: ASM-20260909-ca7dfd
            text: Harbor Pay is a licensed money transmitter subject to state MTL
              regimes and federal BSA/AML obligations (not yet confirmed).
            reason: Needed to continue
          - assumption_id: ASM-20260909-d40239
            text: Mosaic Relay is itself a licensed money transmitter in 41 states
              (per the request).
            reason: Needed to continue
          - assumption_id: ASM-20260909-57e86b
            text: The acquisition is structured as an asset or stock purchase, which
              affects whether Harbor's licenses and SAR/alert obligations transfer
              (structure not yet confirmed).
            reason: Needed to continue
          questions:
          - question_id: Q-SAR
            text: Have the ~40 open alerts and the handful of SARs from the last year
              been reviewed, and what did they reveal?
            consequence: Unread open alerts and SARs are the highest-risk item; they
              can create BSA/AML exposure that must be addressed before close and
              could affect the deal itself.
            state: answered
            answer: The open alerts and SAR history have not been reviewed and remain
              in diligence.
            answer_kind: reported_fact
            linked_fact_ids: &id022
            - FACT-20260909-070aec
          - question_id: Q-KYC
            text: For the pre-2023 Harbor users with no underlying KYC documents,
              what is the plan to satisfy Mosaic Relay's own BSA/AML customer identification
              and recordkeeping obligations?
            consequence: Whether the missing underlying documents create a compliance
              gap depends on how Mosaic plans to satisfy its own CIP/recordkeeping
              obligations for these users.
            state: answered
            answer: It is not yet known whether Mosaic Relay's AML/CIP program permits
              reliance on Harbor's prior KYC; compliance must confirm.
            answer_kind: reported_fact
            linked_fact_ids: &id023
            - FACT-20260909-620c62
          - question_id: Q-MTL
            text: For the four new markets opened by the combined license footprint,
              what is the plan for state money transmission licensing or approval
              before onboarding those customers?
            consequence: Whether the four new markets can be served at close depends
              on license transferability and state approval, which is a gating regulatory
              question.
            state: answered
            answer: Mosaic Relay currently plans to use or assume Harbor's licenses
              for the four new markets.
            answer_kind: reported_fact
            linked_fact_ids: &id024
            - FACT-20260909-771ee1
          - question_id: Q-BONUS
            text: How is the $50 welcome bonus structured?
            consequence: The structure determines whether the bonus triggers consumer
              disclosure, funds-flow, or state promotional requirements.
            state: left_open
            answer: null
            answer_kind: null
            linked_fact_ids: &id025 []
          context: &id026

              # original_request_historical_not_current_facts: original_request

              # Original Request


              From: Dana, PM — Growth & Integration

              Subject: Harbor migration — anything here need legal?


              We''re acquiring Harbor Pay. Close is targeted Nov 15 (~10 weeks). Migration
              plan:


              240k consumer users, 12k business accounts move into our app on day
              1 post-close. One unified account, one balance, full access to our product
              set.

              No re-verification — they were already KYC''d by Harbor.

              Harbor holds MTLs in 38 states, we hold 41. Combined that''s 44 — nice
              win, opens four new markets.

              Harbor''s KYC ran through a vendor that shut down in 2024. We have pass/fail
              results for everyone, but not the underlying documents for users onboarded
              before 2023.

              Harbor''s monitoring is a third-party SaaS whose contract terminates
              at close. Plan is to repoint their transaction feed at our engine on
              day 1.

              Marketing wants a "Welcome" campaign with a $50 bonus on first transfer,
              launching close+1.

              Diligence flagged that Harbor has ~40 open alerts and "a handful" of
              SARs from the last year. Nobody''s read them yet.


              Does any of this need legal before close? It''s really just a data migration.



              ---

              # company.md

              # Company profile


              ## Company Name


              Mosaic Relay


              ## Website Url


              Not provided.


              ## Summary


              Mosaic Relay is a fictional payment infrastructure company created only
              for this Counsel OS UX experiment. It has no real-company affiliation
              and must not be confused with Stripe, Checkout.com, Marqeta, Manifest
              Financial, Chime, or any other real company. Business model: Mosaic
              Relay sells API and hosted infrastructure to online businesses, marketplaces,
              and software platforms. It charges platform and usage fees for payment
              acceptance, risk, operations, and payout tools. It is not a bank. It
              does not offer bank accounts, hold deposits, take deposits, or operate
              as a bank. Products and services: APIs and hosted tools for card, ACH,
              and local payment acceptance; payment routing and splits; refunds and
              disputes; seller, contractor, and recipient payouts; merchant onboarding;
              identity verification; KYB and KYC; sanctions screening; fraud monitoring;
              transaction risk scoring; payout controls; reconciliation; and operations
              dashboards. Customers: online businesses, marketplaces, and software
              platforms, including businesses that onboard sellers, contractors, or
              recipients. Mosaic Relay supports their payment operations but does
              not take custody of customer deposits. Partners and dependencies: acquiring
              banks, payment processors, card networks, identity and fraud vendors,
              and cloud providers. These are third-party relationships, not ownership
              or affiliation. Footprint and teams: fictional US-headquartered company
              serving customers in North America, the UK, EU, and selected other markets
              through remote and regional teams. Core teams are product and engineering,
              payments operations, risk and trust, compliance and financial crime,
              sales and customer success, security and privacy, finance, and legal.
              Legal topics: BSA/AML and financial-crime controls; money transmission
              and payments regulation; card-network and processor rules; ACH and local-payment
              rules; consumer protection and funds-flow disclosures; privacy and data
              protection; cybersecurity and incident response; sanctions; KYB/KYC
              and identity; vendor and cloud contracts; commercial terms; IP; employment;
              and marketing compliance. Risk posture: moderate-to-high regulatory
              and operational risk because the platform supports payment acceptance,
              onboarding, risk decisions, and payouts. Mosaic Relay uses risk-based
              controls, clear ownership, documented escalation, auditability, vendor
              oversight, data minimization, and human review for material exceptions.
              It aims to prevent fraud, sanctions breaches, unsafe payouts, and misleading
              customer claims while keeping the product usable. Operating principles:
              be accurate about what Mosaic Relay does and does not do; separate recommendations
              from recorded decisions; state material assumptions; preserve an editable
              work product; avoid claiming licenses, registrations, approvals, or
              bank status unless confirmed; protect personal data; and favor a useful
              first pass with clear gaps over a blank result. Fictional legal owner:
              Jordan Lee, fictional General Counsel and Head of Regulatory Affairs
              for Mosaic Relay. Jordan owns legal decisions and escalation. The company,
              people, partners, customers, and footprint described here are fictional
              for testing.


              ## Business Model


              Sells API and hosted infrastructure to online businesses, marketplaces,
              and software platforms. Charges platform and usage fees for payment
              acceptance, risk, operations, and payout tools. Not a bank; does not
              hold or take deposits.


              ## Products Services


              APIs and hosted tools for card, ACH, and local payment acceptance; payment
              routing and splits; refunds and disputes; seller, contractor, and recipient
              payouts; merchant onboarding; identity verification; KYB and KYC; sanctions
              screening; fraud monitoring; transaction risk scoring; payout controls;
              reconciliation; and operations dashboards.


              ## Jurisdictions


              US-headquartered; serves customers in North America, the UK, EU, and
              selected other markets through remote and regional teams.


              ## Regulatory Context


              Subject to BSA/AML and financial-crime controls; money transmission
              and payments regulation; card-network and processor rules; ACH and local-payment
              rules; consumer protection and funds-flow disclosures; privacy and data
              protection; cybersecurity and incident response; sanctions; KYB/KYC
              and identity; vendor and cloud contracts; commercial terms; IP; employment;
              and marketing compliance.


              ## Data Practices


              Processes personal data in connection with identity verification, KYB/KYC,
              sanctions screening, fraud monitoring, and transaction risk scoring.
              Emphasizes data minimization and protection of personal data.


              ## Risk Posture


              Moderate-to-high regulatory and operational risk due to payment acceptance,
              onboarding, risk decisions, and payouts. Uses risk-based controls, clear
              ownership, documented escalation, auditability, vendor oversight, data
              minimization, and human review for material exceptions. Aims to prevent
              fraud, sanctions breaches, unsafe payouts, and misleading customer claims
              while keeping the product usable.

        inputs:
          business_question: *id001
          issue:
            issue_id: ISS-c9d7c39f63-ca95e529feeb1045
            title: 'BSA/AML and KYC: whether relying on Harbor''s prior KYC (with
              missing underlying documents for pre-2023 users) satisfies Mosaic Relay''s
              independent CIP/AML obligations'
            why_it_matters: ''
            parent_issue_id: null
            fact_ids: []
          facts:
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-182bcc
            text: Mosaic Relay is acquiring Harbor Pay; close targeted Nov 15 (~10
              weeks out).
            source_ids: *id002
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-551367
            text: Migration moves 240k consumer users and 12k business accounts into
              Mosaic Relay's app on day 1 post-close, with one unified account and
              balance and full access to Mosaic's product set.
            source_ids: *id003
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-9a0c61
            text: Plan is no re-verification; Harbor users were already KYC'd by Harbor.
            source_ids: *id004
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-33293c
            text: Harbor holds money transmitter licenses (MTLs) in 38 states; Mosaic
              Relay holds 41; combined footprint is 44 states, opening four new markets.
            source_ids: *id005
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-aea8ec
            text: Harbor's KYC ran through a vendor that shut down in 2024; Mosaic
              has pass/fail results for everyone but not the underlying documents
              for users onboarded before 2023.
            source_ids: *id006
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-073838
            text: Harbor's transaction monitoring is a third-party SaaS whose contract
              terminates at close; plan is to repoint Harbor's transaction feed at
              Mosaic's engine on day 1.
            source_ids: *id007
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-19aebc
            text: Marketing wants a 'Welcome' campaign with a $50 bonus on first transfer,
              launching close+1.
            source_ids: *id008
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-adf2cb
            text: Diligence flagged ~40 open alerts and 'a handful' of SARs from the
              last year at Harbor; nobody has read them yet.
            source_ids: *id009
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-4c1fd8
            text: Migration moves 240k consumer users and 12k business accounts into
              Mosaic Relay's app on day 1 post-close, with one unified account and
              balance.
            source_ids: *id010
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-f2c49f
            text: Plan is no re-verification of Harbor's existing KYC; Harbor users
              were already KYC'd.
            source_ids: *id011
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-13d4c1
            text: Harbor's KYC ran through a vendor that shut down in 2024; pass/fail
              results exist for everyone but underlying documents are missing for
              users onboarded before 2023.
            source_ids: *id012
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-78d5cd
            text: Harbor's transaction monitoring is a third-party SaaS whose contract
              terminates at close; plan is to repoint the transaction feed to Mosaic
              Relay's engine on day 1.
            source_ids: *id013
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-9f7bde
            text: Diligence flagged ~40 open alerts and 'a handful' of SARs from the
              last year at Harbor; none have been read yet.
            source_ids: *id014
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-ed9f06
            text: How is the Harbor Pay acquisition structured? — Asset purchase (Mosaic
              buys Harbor's assets)
            source_ids: *id015
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-b5e71e
            text: Does Mosaic Relay's AML/CIP program currently permit relying on
              another institution's prior KYC without re-verification, or is re-verification
              required? — Not sure / need to check with compliance
            source_ids: *id016
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-849b00
            text: What is known about Harbor's ~40 open alerts and the SARs from the
              last year? — Nothing reviewed yet — still in diligence
            source_ids: *id017
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-f0fb01
            text: For the four new markets opened by the combined footprint, does
              Mosaic Relay plan to operate under Harbor's existing licenses or apply
              for its own? — Use/assume Harbor's licenses
            source_ids: *id018
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-620c62
            text: For the pre-2023 Harbor users with no underlying KYC documents,
              what is the plan to satisfy Mosaic Relay's own BSA/AML customer identification
              and recordkeeping obligations? — It is not yet known whether Mosaic
              Relay's AML/CIP program permits reliance on Harbor's prior KYC; compliance
              must confirm.
            source_ids: *id019
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-070aec
            text: Have the ~40 open alerts and the handful of SARs from the last year
              been reviewed, and what did they reveal? — The open alerts and SAR history
              have not been reviewed and remain in diligence.
            source_ids: *id020
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-771ee1
            text: For the four new markets opened by the combined license footprint,
              what is the plan for state money transmission licensing or approval
              before onboarding those customers? — Mosaic Relay currently plans to
              use or assume Harbor's licenses for the four new markets.
            source_ids: *id021
          assumptions:
          - assumption_id: ASM-20260909-29b823
            text: Mosaic Relay's existing money transmission licenses and Harbor's
              MTLs are transferable or can be maintained through the acquisition without
              new state approval
            reason: Needed to continue
          - assumption_id: ASM-20260909-3c5756
            text: The KYC pass/fail results are sufficient to satisfy Mosaic Relay's
              own BSA/AML program obligations without the underlying documents
            reason: Needed to continue
          - assumption_id: ASM-20260909-1a519f
            text: The transaction monitoring repoint can be completed and validated
              before close without a monitoring gap
            reason: Needed to continue
          - assumption_id: ASM-20260909-ca7dfd
            text: Harbor Pay is a licensed money transmitter subject to state MTL
              regimes and federal BSA/AML obligations (not yet confirmed).
            reason: Needed to continue
          - assumption_id: ASM-20260909-d40239
            text: Mosaic Relay is itself a licensed money transmitter in 41 states
              (per the request).
            reason: Needed to continue
          - assumption_id: ASM-20260909-57e86b
            text: The acquisition is structured as an asset or stock purchase, which
              affects whether Harbor's licenses and SAR/alert obligations transfer
              (structure not yet confirmed).
            reason: Needed to continue
          questions:
          - question_id: Q-SAR
            text: Have the ~40 open alerts and the handful of SARs from the last year
              been reviewed, and what did they reveal?
            consequence: Unread open alerts and SARs are the highest-risk item; they
              can create BSA/AML exposure that must be addressed before close and
              could affect the deal itself.
            state: answered
            answer: The open alerts and SAR history have not been reviewed and remain
              in diligence.
            answer_kind: reported_fact
            linked_fact_ids: *id022
          - question_id: Q-KYC
            text: For the pre-2023 Harbor users with no underlying KYC documents,
              what is the plan to satisfy Mosaic Relay's own BSA/AML customer identification
              and recordkeeping obligations?
            consequence: Whether the missing underlying documents create a compliance
              gap depends on how Mosaic plans to satisfy its own CIP/recordkeeping
              obligations for these users.
            state: answered
            answer: It is not yet known whether Mosaic Relay's AML/CIP program permits
              reliance on Harbor's prior KYC; compliance must confirm.
            answer_kind: reported_fact
            linked_fact_ids: *id023
          - question_id: Q-MTL
            text: For the four new markets opened by the combined license footprint,
              what is the plan for state money transmission licensing or approval
              before onboarding those customers?
            consequence: Whether the four new markets can be served at close depends
              on license transferability and state approval, which is a gating regulatory
              question.
            state: answered
            answer: Mosaic Relay currently plans to use or assume Harbor's licenses
              for the four new markets.
            answer_kind: reported_fact
            linked_fact_ids: *id024
          - question_id: Q-BONUS
            text: How is the $50 welcome bonus structured?
            consequence: The structure determines whether the bonus triggers consumer
              disclosure, funds-flow, or state promotional requirements.
            state: left_open
            answer: null
            answer_kind: null
            linked_fact_ids: *id025
          context: *id026
        prior_reference: null

            text: Mosaic Relay is itself a licensed money transmitter in 41 states
              (per the request).
            reason: Needed to continue
          - assumption_id: ASM-20260909-57e86b
            text: The acquisition is structured as an asset or stock purchase, which

          questions:
          - question_id: Q-SAR
            text: Have the ~40 open alerts and the handful of SARs from the last year
              been reviewed, and what did they reveal?
            consequence: Unread open alerts and SARs are the highest-risk item; they
              can create BSA/AML exposure that must be addressed before close and

        inputs:
          business_question: *id001
          issue:
            issue_id: ISS-c9d7c39f63-4825f0af7ec75542
            title: 'Monitoring vendor transition: continuity of transaction monitoring
              when the SaaS contract terminates at close'
            why_it_matters: ''
            parent_issue_id: null
            fact_ids: []
          facts:
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-182bcc
            text: Mosaic Relay is acquiring Harbor Pay; close targeted Nov 15 (~10
              weeks out).
            source_ids: *id002
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-551367
            text: Migration moves 240k consumer users and 12k business accounts into
              Mosaic Relay's app on day 1 post-close, with one unified account and
              balance and full access to Mosaic's product set.
            source_ids: *id003
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-9a0c61
            text: Plan is no re-verification; Harbor users were already KYC'd by Harbor.
            source_ids: *id004
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-33293c
            text: Harbor holds money transmitter licenses (MTLs) in 38 states; Mosaic
              Relay holds 41; combined footprint is 44 states, opening four new markets.
            source_ids: *id005
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-aea8ec
            text: Harbor's KYC ran through a vendor that shut down in 2024; Mosaic
              has pass/fail results for everyone but not the underlying documents
              for users onboarded before 2023.
            source_ids: *id006
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-073838
            text: Harbor's transaction monitoring is a third-party SaaS whose contract
              terminates at close; plan is to repoint Harbor's transaction feed at
              Mosaic's engine on day 1.
            source_ids: *id007
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-19aebc
            text: Marketing wants a 'Welcome' campaign with a $50 bonus on first transfer,
              launching close+1.
            source_ids: *id008
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-adf2cb
            text: Diligence flagged ~40 open alerts and 'a handful' of SARs from the
              last year at Harbor; nobody has read them yet.
            source_ids: *id009
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-4c1fd8
            text: Migration moves 240k consumer users and 12k business accounts into
              Mosaic Relay's app on day 1 post-close, with one unified account and
              balance.
            source_ids: *id010
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-f2c49f
            text: Plan is no re-verification of Harbor's existing KYC; Harbor users
              were already KYC'd.
            source_ids: *id011
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-13d4c1
            text: Harbor's KYC ran through a vendor that shut down in 2024; pass/fail
              results exist for everyone but underlying documents are missing for
              users onboarded before 2023.
            source_ids: *id012
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-78d5cd
            text: Harbor's transaction monitoring is a third-party SaaS whose contract
              terminates at close; plan is to repoint the transaction feed to Mosaic
              Relay's engine on day 1.
            source_ids: *id013
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-9f7bde
            text: Diligence flagged ~40 open alerts and 'a handful' of SARs from the
              last year at Harbor; none have been read yet.
            source_ids: *id014
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-ed9f06
            text: How is the Harbor Pay acquisition structured? — Asset purchase (Mosaic
              buys Harbor's assets)
            source_ids: *id015
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-b5e71e
            text: Does Mosaic Relay's AML/CIP program currently permit relying on
              another institution's prior KYC without re-verification, or is re-verification
              required? — Not sure / need to check with compliance
            source_ids: *id016
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-849b00
            text: What is known about Harbor's ~40 open alerts and the SARs from the
              last year? — Nothing reviewed yet — still in diligence
            source_ids: *id017
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-f0fb01
            text: For the four new markets opened by the combined footprint, does
              Mosaic Relay plan to operate under Harbor's existing licenses or apply
              for its own? — Use/assume Harbor's licenses
            source_ids: *id018
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-620c62
            text: For the pre-2023 Harbor users with no underlying KYC documents,
              what is the plan to satisfy Mosaic Relay's own BSA/AML customer identification
              and recordkeeping obligations? — It is not yet known whether Mosaic
              Relay's AML/CIP program permits reliance on Harbor's prior KYC; compliance
              must confirm.
            source_ids: *id019
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-070aec
            text: Have the ~40 open alerts and the handful of SARs from the last year
              been reviewed, and what did they reveal? — The open alerts and SAR history
              have not been reviewed and remain in diligence.
            source_ids: *id020
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-771ee1
            text: For the four new markets opened by the combined license footprint,
              what is the plan for state money transmission licensing or approval
              before onboarding those customers? — Mosaic Relay currently plans to
              use or assume Harbor's licenses for the four new markets.
            source_ids: *id021
          assumptions:
          - assumption_id: ASM-20260909-29b823
            text: Mosaic Relay's existing money transmission licenses and Harbor's
              MTLs are transferable or can be maintained through the acquisition without

            reason: Needed to continue
          - assumption_id: ASM-20260909-3c5756

          - assumption_id: ASM-20260909-1a519f

            reason: Needed to continue
          - assumption_id: ASM-20260909-ca7dfd
            text: Harbor Pay is a licensed money transmitter subject to state MTL

            reason: Needed to continue
          - assumption_id: ASM-20260909-d40239
            text: Mosaic Relay is itself a licensed money transmitter in 41 states

            reason: Needed to continue

            text: The acquisition is structured as an asset or stock purchase, which

            state: answered

            answer_kind: reported_fact
            linked_fact_ids: *id023
          - question_id: Q-MTL
            text: For the four new markets opened by the combined license footprint,
              what is the plan for state money transmission licensing or approval

            consequence: Whether the four new markets can be served at close depends

            state: answered
            answer: Mosaic Relay currently plans to use or assume Harbor's licenses

            answer_kind: reported_fact
            linked_fact_ids: *id024
          - question_id: Q-BONUS
            text: How is the $50 welcome bonus structured?
            consequence: The structure determines whether the bonus triggers consumer

        title: 'Money transmission licensing: whether Mosaic Relay can operate under
          the combined 44-state footprint and whether Harbor''s MTLs transfer/survive

        title: 'BSA/AML and KYC: whether relying on Harbor''s prior KYC (with missing

        why_it_matters: ''

        fact_ids: []
        disposition: null
      - issue_id: ISS-c9d7c39f63-69af3f282d21ef38
        title: 'SAR and alert handling: obligations to review, file, and manage Harbor''s
          ~40 open alerts and SARs, and successor liability'
        why_it_matters: ''

        title: 'Monitoring vendor transition: continuity of transaction monitoring

      - issue_id: ISS-c9d7c39f63-72810d0a43d3ce66
        title: 'Marketing compliance: the $50 welcome bonus offer and any state money

        why_it_matters: ''
        parent_issue_id: null
        fact_ids: []
        disposition: null
      questions:
      - question_id: Q-SAR
        text: Have the ~40 open alerts and the handful of SARs from the last year
          been reviewed, and what did they reveal?
        consequence: Unread open alerts and SARs are the highest-risk item; they can
          create BSA/AML exposure that must be addressed before close and could affect
          the deal itself.
        state: answered
        answer: The open alerts and SAR history have not been reviewed and remain
          in diligence.
        answer_kind: reported_fact
        linked_fact_ids:
        - FACT-20260909-070aec
      - question_id: Q-KYC
        text: For the pre-2023 Harbor users with no underlying KYC documents, what
          is the plan to satisfy Mosaic Relay's own BSA/AML customer identification
          and recordkeeping obligations?
        consequence: Whether the missing underlying documents create a compliance
          gap depends on how Mosaic plans to satisfy its own CIP/recordkeeping obligations
          for these users.
        state: answered
        answer: It is not yet known whether Mosaic Relay's AML/CIP program permits
          reliance on Harbor's prior KYC; compliance must confirm.
        answer_kind: reported_fact
        linked_fact_ids:
        - FACT-20260909-620c62
      - question_id: Q-MTL
        text: For the four new markets opened by the combined license footprint, what
          is the plan for state money transmission licensing or approval before onboarding
          those customers?
        consequence: Whether the four new markets can be served at close depends on
          license transferability and state approval, which is a gating regulatory
          question.
        state: answered
        answer: Mosaic Relay currently plans to use or assume Harbor's licenses for
          the four new markets.
        answer_kind: reported_fact
        linked_fact_ids:
        - FACT-20260909-771ee1
      - question_id: Q-BONUS
        text: How is the $50 welcome bonus structured?
        consequence: The structure determines whether the bonus triggers consumer
          disclosure, funds-flow, or state promotional requirements.
        state: left_open
        answer: null
        answer_kind: null
        linked_fact_ids: []
      decisions: []
